Algorithm "active flattening":
- p sum1 positive.
- Repeat:
  1. c=p*p (FFT).
  2. Choose active set A: lags with c_k >= max( (1-ε) max_c, maybe quantile), or top L e.g. L=ceil(0.2*(2n-1))? y uniform over A.
  3. Compute h_i = ∑_{k∈A} y_k p_{k-i} = (1/|A|) ∑_{k∈A} p_{k-i}. This is cross-correlation of indicator A with p.
  4. Update p_i ← p_i * h_i^{-α} or exp(-η h_i / mean), then normalize and clip.
At optimum h_i=T (constant). If h_i high, reduce p_i. This is like solving p*y=const. But will it also reduce c? Since y supported on high c lags, reducing contributions to those lags should lower max.

```
def next_pow2(m):
    p=1
    while p<m: p*=2
    return p
```

- n=len(a), m=len(b); L=pow2(n+m-1). If L > e.g. 1<<24 maybe; but n <= maybe 10000 L=32768 ok.
- fa=np.fft.rfft(a, L); fb=np.fft.rfft(b,L); c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fb,L)[:n+m-1]
- due roundoff, small negative; clip 0.
For square, same fa.

Let's design `soft_refine(p, time_limit, rng, beta0, beta1, gamma, use_self=True)`:
Inputs p float array. Output best_p, best_score.
Pseudo:
```
def soft_refine(p, time_limit=..., beta_min=..., beta_max=..., verbose=True):
    start=time.monotonic(); best=...; p=current normalized
    n=len(p); K=2*n-1
    L = _fft_len_square(n) maybe cache? No global cache? simple.
    it=0
    # phase 1: adaptive beta
    n_iter_estimate = maybe 10000 or until time. Use while time.
    gamma_init = 0.3, gamma_adapt? 
    for beta_factor in schedule? Simpler: linearly increase beta = beta_min + (beta_max-beta_min)*(elapsed/time)^2.
    # maintain logp to avoid underflow. logp = np.log(p+1e-300); subtract max.
    logp = np.log(p) - logp.max()
    while time < limit:
        p = np.exp(logp - logp.max()); p /= p.sum()
        c = fft_square(p)
        maxc = c.max(); score=2*n*maxc
        update best
        beta = ...
        # weights
        diff = c - maxc
        # if beta small, use linear weights w = 1+ alpha*(c/mean?)? softmax can underflow.
        maxdiff=diff.max()
        logw = beta*diff - beta*maxdiff? Actually diff≤0. logw = beta*(c - maxc). max 0.
        logw -= logw.max(); w=exp(logw); w/=w.sum()
        h = fft_corr_valid(w,p)
        h = np.maximum(h,1e-300)
        # update logp: logp += -gamma*(log(h)-medlog) + noise?
        med = np.median(h) # or mean
        ratio = h/med
        # clip log ratio
        lr = np.log(ratio)
        clipval = max_step (e.g. 0.5)
        lr = np.clip(lr, -clipval, clipval)
        logp -= gamma*lr
        # remove mean logp to avoid scale drift: logp -= logp.mean()*p? Better subtract mean: logp -= np.mean(logp)
        # maybe add noise to escape: if accepted? 
        # adapt gamma: if score stuck, reduce/increase
    return best
```
But use `np.median` each iter O(n log n) if n large; maybe use mean or 25th percentile? We want normalize h around its mean. Use `h_avg = h.mean()`. Mean O(n). ratio may have zeros; use eps. log ratio. For h_i high reduce p_i. If ratio variance high, log ratio large. Use `np.clip`. Good.

Potential issue: If active weights soft concentrate, h may have zeros for some i (if no active lag includes i). For p positive all, if active set nonempty, h_i may be zero if no k ∈ A with i∈[0,k]? For i near n-1 and active low lags? But active high lags likely central, so some. Use eps.

Should we subtract mean logp? If logp updated by -γ log(h/mean), sum log changes; p normalization then exp and sum. If logp drifts, no problem if normalize. But large drift may overflow; subtract max before exp. Use `logp -= np.mean(logp)` to keep center.

Step size gamma: Since log update normalized, gamma can be 0.1 to 1. If gamma too high, may oscillate. We can anneal: start gamma=0.5, decrease to 0.1. Or use backtracking: after update compute new score; if score worsens, rollback and reduce gamma. Since objective nonconvex, accepting worsening may help escape but for minimizing, maybe accept small? We can use stochastic: with probability accept if new_score < best or < current + temp. But for reliability, maybe use hill-climbing with small steps; escape via random perturbations.

We can maintain current p and best p. At each iter, if new_score <= current_score + tiny accept; else maybe if score > current but < best + temp? Could do simulated annealing: accept if smaller or with prob exp((current-new)/T). But too much time. Maybe period perturb best and restart.

Alternative: use "line search" on gamma: Try gamma sequence, choose best among several? Expensive.

The soft gradient with logh may not monotonically improve. We can combine with simple local Search:
- After some iterations, take best and do `local_block_improve`: random block multiply by factor; maybe coordinate adjust. But maybe gradient enough.

Let's consider `cross_refine` method more. For current f, solve g min max(f*g). Convex; we can use similar logh update but mult indices with f not g:
```
def solve_cross(f, g_init, time_limit, beta):
   f norm
   g = g_init norm
   while time:
      c=fft_convolve(f,g)
      maxc, score_cross
      w=softmax(beta c)
      h=fft_corr_valid(w, f)  # gradient wrt g
      g *= exp(-γ(log(h/hmean))) normalize
   return g
```
Then mixture f_new=(1-t)f + t g. Check self score; accept if improves. This inner solve might be costly but promises directions. We can run a limited number of cross directions (e.g., 10-20) per candidate. Each cross solve maybe 100-500 iterations. Since f fixed, objective convex, maybe converges. But solving for each direction with many f same? Could reuse. Step size maybe.

Maybe direct self soft descent is sufficient; cross adds complexity. But previous LP direction likely robust; we can implement a simpler direction via `solve_cross` and mixture. Need ensure no bugs.

Could also solve for g as the minimizer of max(f*g) using `convex_log_descent` with more iterations; then if g≠f, mixing may reduce. This is like "Whittle" recursive. Let's design robust soft descent with active h and hill climbing.

Pseudo for `optimize_logp`:
```
def optimize_logp(p0, time_limit, rng, beta_min, beta_max, gamma_init, verbose):
   p=normalized p0
   best=p, best_score=score_fft(p)
   logp=np.log(p+1e-30)
   logp-=logp.max()
   start=monotonic(); stagnant=0
   gamma=gamma_init
   last_improve=0
   while time < limit:
      p=np.exp(logp); p/=sum
      c=conv_sq(p); maxc=c.max(); score=2*n*maxc
      if score < best_score: best...
      # compute weights
      beta = schedule based on elapsed fraction: beta_min + (beta_max-beta_min)*t^2
      # weights soft; if beta large, use top with soft.
      c_rel = c - maxc
      # To avoid all underflow, use linear if beta*range small? Just do logw = beta*c_rel; logw-=max(0)
      # w = exp(logw); w/=sum
      # Instead of full softmax, could w = np.maximum(0, c_rel + range) / sum? Hmm.
      h = corr(w,p)
      h = np.maximum(h, tight eps)
      meanh=h.mean()
      # update direction: log_ratio = np.log(h/meanh)
      # choose step adaptively: maybe target maximum relative change.
      lr = np.log(h/meanh)
      # If lr variance too high, scale down: lr = lr * min(1, 0.5/std)?
      # Use backtracking: try gamma in [gamma, gamma/2,...] and pick best new score? Expensive but can do small set.
      new_logp = logp - gamma*lr
      new_logp -= new_logp.mean()
      new_p = softmax(new_logp)
      new_score = score_fft(new_p)
      # accept if improves current or best? We may want accept uphill occasionally.
      if new_score <= current_score*(1+1e-12) or rng.random()<anneal_prob:
          logp=new_logp; current_score=new_score
      else:
          gamma *=0.5 (min)
          # maybe try smaller until accept
      # gamma adapt: if improved, gamma*=1.05; if stagnant, perturb.
   # periodic random perturb in logp from best
   return best
```

Active set update:
```
delta = 0.5 * (0.5 ->0.01) or based on quantile. active = c >= maxc - delta
h = correlate_valid(active, p) / active.sum()
# h zero for some; add eps * mean.
lr = log(h/meanh)
logp -= gamma*clip(lr)
```
Potential: if active set sparse (few lags), h may be zero for some coordinates. But p positive; if a coordinate doesn't participate in any active lag? All coordinates participate in some lag k in active (if active nonempty, for each i, need k=i+j). If active lags arbitrary, maybe not. Use eps.

Which update reduces max? If active uniform high lags, we reduce p_i if high h. It enforces h constant. If h variation due active set, good.

We can alternate with direct gradient: `logp -= gamma2 * (h - meanh)` (linear) maybe for small variations. But log ratio can be large when h near zero. Use both: update = -γ*(h/meanh -1) clipped. This is simpler and avoids log zeros. First-order same. Let's use linear normalized. `delta_h = h/meanh - 1`; clip to [-1,1]; logp -= γ*delta_h`. This might be more stable. If h_i=0, delta=-1, increases p_i (if below mean) to fill gaps. But log update with zeros would -∞? Actually log(h/mean) → -∞, so logp increases infinite, p_i grows; linear caps at γ. Linear better. Which matches KKT? For h near T+δ, delta≈δ/T. Good. Use linear.

Update p via logp: logp +=? If h high, reduce logp: logp -= γ*(h/meanh -1). If h=0, increase by γ. Clip. Normalize. This is scale invariant and no eps. If h all zeros (bad), no change.

This is essentially multiplicative gradient. Let's test n=2 optimal [2/3,1/3] active {0,1}, h=[0.5,0.333], mean=0.416, delta=[0.2, -0.2]. logp decrease p0, increase p1 -> [1/3,2/3]. Same score after reflection. Good.

The h uses current; update can be accepted blindly. Then next iteration c computed. At the end of each iteration we can compute score from c. We don't need extra score after update; just update logp. But if update diverges, next c will be bad; can rollback by storing previous logp if next score worse than best? We can store previous p and if score increases too much, revert to previous and reduce gamma. That uses stale score? Let's design:
- At loop start, p from logp, c,score.
- If score < best, update best and save best_logp.
- Compute h from c.
- Update logp to new_logp with current gamma.
- Before next loop, we don't know score. Next loop compute p_new, c_new, score_new. If score_new > score_current + margin and > best_score? Could revert to logp_prev (or best) and reduce gamma. But we may have already updated. This is okay; no extra conv.
- Maintain gamma: if score_new <= score_current, gamma *=1.05 up to max; else gamma *=0.7; if score_new > best_score + 0.05 maybe reset logp to best, gamma small, perturb.

Implementation of `optimize_sequence` in detail:
```
def optimize_sequence(p0, time_limit, rng, tag='', verbose=True):
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+time_limit
    n=len(p0)
    p = np.asarray(p0, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    p = np.maximum(p,0)
    if p.sum()<1e-12: p=np.ones(n); p/=n
    else: p/=p.sum()
    # ensure positive floor
    floor = 1e-12 / n? relative maybe
    p = p + 1e-12
    p/=p.sum()
    L = next_power_of_two(3*n-2)
    best_p = p.copy(); best_score = score_fft(p)
    logp = np.log(p) - np.log(p).max()
    best_logp = logp.copy()
    gamma = 0.3
    min_gamma=0.02; max_gamma=1.5
    prev_score = best_score
    stale = 0
    last_perturb=0
    iters=0
    phase_time = ...
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
        p = np.exp(logp - np.max(logp)); p/=p.sum()
        Fp = np.fft.rfft(p,L)
        c = np.fft.irfft(Fp*Fp, L)[:2*n-1]
        c = np.maximum(c,0)
        maxc = c.max()
        score = 2*n*maxc  # sum=1
        iters+=1
        if score < best_score:
             best_score=score; best_p=p.copy(); best_logp=logp.copy(); stale=0
             if verbose and (iters%10==0 or score<best_score-1e-6): print
        else:
             stale +=1
        # adapt gamma
        if prev_score is not None:
             if score < prev_score - 1e-12: gamma=min(max_gamma,gamma*1.04)
             elif score > prev_score + 1e-12: gamma=max(min_gamma,gamma*0.85)
             if score > best_score + 0.02:
                    # reset
                    logp = best_logp.copy()
                    # add small noise
                    sigma=0.02
                    logp += rng.normal(0,sigma,n)
                    gamma=0.2
             prev_score=score
        # choose active
        frac = min(1.0,(time.monotonic()-start)/time_limit)
        # mode: first half hard, second half soft?
        if frac < 0.6:
            delta = 0.5 - 0.48*frac/0.6 # 0.5 to .02
            w = compute_active_hard(c, delta, min_active=max(3, (2*n-1)//20))
            w = w / w.sum()
        else:
            tf = 5 + 195*((frac-0.6)/0.4)**2 # 5 to 200
            logw = (c - maxc)*tf
            logw -= np.max(logw) # 0
            w=np.exp(logw); s=w.sum(); if s<1e-300: w=np.ones(2n-1)/K else w/=s
        h = corr_valid_fft(w,p,L)  # w sum1
        # normalize h? It should be approx? h values maybe scale? For w sum1, h_i average convolution of p? h mean = sum_i p_i h_i? =∑ p_i∑_j p_j w_{i+j}=∑ c_k w_k ≈ maxc. So mean ~ maxc. Good. h_mean = h.mean() maybe not exactly; use h_mean = h.mean()
        if h_mean <= 0 or not finite: break
        delta_h = h / h_mean - 1.0
        # For stability, clip to [-2,2]
        np.clip(delta_h, -1.0, 1.0, out=delta_h)
        # Add tiny random noise? maybe if stale
        if stale > max(50, iters//10):
             # perturb
             sigma = 0.05 * (1.0 - min(1.0,stale/1000)) +0.01
             logp = best_logp.copy()
             logp += rng.normal(0, sigma, n)
             gamma = 0.2
             stale=0
             # skip update this iter? continue to recompute
             continue
        logp = logp - gamma * delta_h
        # renormalize logp
        logp -= np.max(logp)  # but we need keep relative; max may shift.
        # floor relative
        max_log = logp.max()
        min_log = max_log - 40.0
        logp = np.maximum(logp, min_log)
        # To avoid drift from normalization, we can subtract weighted? p normalization in next iter handles, but logp base can drift. Subtract mean? Since exp normalized, constant irrelevant. Do logp -= np.mean(logp) maybe.
        logp -= np.mean(logp)
        # If p would have zeros, max/min maintained.
    # After loop, maybe final local if n<=1500 and time? Could outer handle.
    return best_p, best_score
```

`soft_refine`: It also can stagnate; uses perturb.

Let's draft `soft_refine` more concretely with parameters:
```
def soft_refine(p0, time_limit, rng, tag='', verbose=True):
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+time_limit
    n=len(p0); L=mp2(3*n-2); K=2*n-1
    p = positive_normalize(p0, floor_rel=1e-12)
    best_p=p.copy(); best_score=score_fft(p,L)
    logp=np.log(p)-np.max(np.log(p))
    best_logp=logp.copy()
    gamma=0.3; min_g=0.02; max_g=1.2
    last_improve=time.monotonic(); it=0
    prev_score=best_score
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
       # p from logp
       p=np.exp(logp-np.max(logp)); p/=p.sum()
       Fp=np.fft.rfft(p,L)
       c=np.fft.irfft(Fp*Fp,L)[:K]
       c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
       maxc=c.max()
       S=p.sum()
       score=2.0*n*maxc/(S*S)
       it+=1
       if score < best_score:
           best_score=score; best_p=p.copy(); best_logp=logp.copy(); last_improve=time.monotonic()
           if verbose and (it<20 or it%100==0): print(f"{tag} soft {it} score={score:.7f}")
       # adapt gamma using prev_score
       if it>1:
          if score < prev_score - 1e-12: gamma=min(max_g,gamma*1.03)
          elif score > prev_score + 1e-12: gamma=max(min_g,gamma*0.9)
          if score > best_score + 0.02:
              # reset to best with noise
              sig=0.05
              logp=best_logp.copy()+rng.normal(0,sig,n)
              gamma=0.2
              prev_score=score? continue
       prev_score=score
       # perturb if stagnant
       dead=time.monotonic()-last_improve
       if dead > max(1.0, 0.1*time_limit) and it>50:
           frac=min(1,(time.monotonic()-start)/time_limit)
           sig=0.15*(1.0-0.7*frac)+0.02
           logp=best_logp.copy()+rng.normal(0,sig,n)
           gamma=0.25
           prev_score=best_score
           last_improve=time.monotonic()
           print(f"{tag} perturbed")
           continue
       # weights
       if maxc > 0:
           frac=min(1.0,(time.monotonic()-start)/time_limit)
           rel_width = 0.25 - 0.23*frac*frac  # 0.25 to 0.02
           # maybe start broad 0.5? 0.25 enough
           logw=(c-maxc)/(rel_width*maxc)
           # if logw has nan, uniform
           logw=np.nan_to_num(logw, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=-100.0)
           w=np.exp(logw)
           w=w+1e-12
           sw=w.sum()
           if sw<=0 or not np.isfinite(sw): w=np.full(K,1.0/K)
           else: w/=sw
       else: w uniform
       h=_corr_valid_from_F(Fp,w,L,n)  # need function using Fp? We computed Fp length L; can use
       if h is None: break
       h=np.maximum(h,1e-300)
       target=float(np.dot(p,h))
       if target<=0: target=h.mean()
       dh=h/target-1.0
       np.clip(dh,-1.0,1.0,out=dh)
       logp = logp - gamma*dh
       logp -= np.max(logp)
       min_log=logp.max()-40.0
       logp=np.maximum(logp,min_log)
       logp -= np.mean(logp)
    return best_p, best_score
```

`_normalize_positive(a, rel_floor=1e-12)`:

```
a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel().copy()

```

```

```

`_fft_square(a, L=None)`:

```
n=len(a); K=2*n-1
if L is None: L=_mp2(max(1,3*n-2)) # use corr length? For score only can use mp2(K) but use corr L for reuse. For local, use L=mp2(3n-2).

return c
```

```
def local_polish(p0, time_limit, rng, tag='', verbose=True):
   n=len(p0)
   if n ==0: return
   L=_mp2(3*n-2)
   p=_normalize_positive(p0,1e-14)
   best_p=p.copy(); best_score=_score_fft(p,L)
   start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+time_limit
   factors=(0.0,0.2,0.4,0.6,0.8,1.1,1.4,1.8,3.0)
   sweeps=0
   while time.monotonic()<deadline:
      sweeps+=1
      improved=False
      if n <= 700:
         # coordinate descent full every sweep maybe expensive; use two sweeps then blocks
         if sweeps % 4 == 1:
           for i in range(n):
              if time>=deadline: break
              orig=best_p[i]
              if orig <=0:
                 continue? Try set small?
              for f in factors:
                 if f==0.0:
                    q=best_p.copy(); q[i]=0.0
                 else:
                    q=best_p.copy(); q[i]=orig*f
                 q=_normalize_positive(q, 1e-15)
                 sc=_score_fft(q,L)
                 if sc < best_score -1e-12:
                    best_score=sc; best_p=q.copy(); improved=True
               # if best changed, orig changes? We use best_p updated; okay but orig old; maybe continue.
      # random block always
      nblocks = min(5000, max(100, int((deadline-time)*20)))
      for _ in range(nblocks):
         if time>=deadline: break
         width = max(1,int(rng.integers(1, max(2, int(0.03*n)+1))))
         start_idx=int(rng.integers(0, max(1,n-width+1)))
         q=best_p.copy()
         # occasionally set to zero if best_p has positive
         if rng.random()<0.1:
             q[start_idx:start_idx+width]=0.0
         else:
             sigma=0.2*max(0.2,1 - (time.monotonic()-start)/time_limit)
             q[start_idx:start_idx+width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,size=width))
         q=max(q,0); q=q/q.sum()
         sc=_score_fft(q,L)
         if sc<best_score:
             best_score=sc; best_p=q.copy(); improved=True
      if not improved and time.monotonic()-last_improve>...??
   return best_p,best_score
```

`_fft_square`:
```
def _fft_square(a, L=None):
    n=a.size; K=2*n-1
    if L is None: L=_mp2(max(1,3*n-2))
    F=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(F*F,L)
    c=c[:K]
    c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
    return c
```
For L=1 and n=1, K=1. Good.

`_interpolate`:
```
def _interpolate(arr, n):
    arr=np.asarray(arr).ravel(); arr=np.maximum(arr,0); arr[~finite]=0
    if arr.size==n: return arr
    x=np.linspace(0,1,arr.size)
    xx=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    return np.interp(xx,x,arr)
```

```python
"""
Search for non-negative step functions minimizing
    2*n*max(conv(a,a)) / sum(a)^2.

The code combines several practical optimization layers:

1. Fast FFT-based scoring and FFT-based gradient/correlation updates.
2. A logarithmic multiplicative descent on the simplex:
      p_i <- p_i * exp(-eta * (h_i / target - 1)),
   where h is the cross-correlation with a smoothed active set of high
   convolution lags.
3. A linearization / cutting-plane style local search:
   - rescale a so that max(a*a)=1,
   - solve an LP maximizing sum(delta) subject to linearized constraints
     (a+delta)*(a+delta) approx a*a + 2a*delta having coefficients <= 1,
   - accept the LP direction only if a line search improves the true score.
4. Random / coordinate / block local polish to remove local stagnation.

The function propose_candidate returned below is the required entry point.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
except Exception:
    linprog = None


def _mp2(m):
    """Smallest power of two >= max(1,m)."""
    m = int(max(1, m))
    p = 1
    while p < m:
        p <<= 1
    return p


def _quick_normalize(a):
    """Max at zero and normalize to sum 1 without adding a floor."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel().copy()
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        a = np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(a.size)
    return a / s


def _normalize_positive(a, rel_floor=1e-12):
    """Normalize to sum 1, clip to nonnegative, and add a tiny relative floor."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel().copy()
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        a = np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(a.size)
    a = a / s
    if rel_floor > 0.0 and a.size > 0:
        a = a + rel_floor
        a = a / a.sum()
    return a


def _init_exact(n):
    """
    Coefficients of (1-x)^(-1/2), i = 0..n-1.
    These saturate the first half of the convolution constraints exactly.
    """
    a = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if n <= 0:
        return a
    a[0] = 1.0
    for i in range(1, n):
        a[i] = a[i - 1] * float(2 * i - 1) / float(2 * i)
    return a


def _init_power(n, exponent=0.5, shift=1.0):
    """Simple power-law sequence."""
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + max(0.5, float(shift))
    a = x ** (-float(exponent))
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    return a


def _init_random_smooth(n, rng):
    """Smooth random sum-of-Gaussians initialization."""
    if n <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    t = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    nb = int(rng.integers(2, 6))
    for _ in range(nb):
        c = float(int(rng.integers(0, n)))
        lo = max(2, n // 20)
        hi = max(lo + 1, n // 4)
        w = float(int(rng.integers(lo, hi)))
        a += np.exp(-0.5 * ((t - c) / w) ** 2)
    if a.sum() <= 1e-15:
        a = _init_exact(n)
    return a


def _interpolate_sequence(arr, n):
    """Linear interpolation to length n."""
    arr = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    arr[~np.isfinite(arr)] = 0.0
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    if arr.size == n:
        return arr.astype(np.float64, copy=True)
    if arr.size <= 0:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if arr.size == 1:
        return np.full(n, float(arr[0]), dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, arr.size)
    xx = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    return np.interp(xx, x, arr).astype(np.float64, copy=False)


def _get_initial(n, kind, prev, rng):
    """Return a sum-1 nonnegative initial vector of length n."""
    if n <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)

    if kind == "exact":
        p = _init_exact(n)
    elif kind == "power":
        p = _init_power(n, 0.5, 1.0)
    elif kind == "random":
        p = _init_random_smooth(n, rng)
    elif kind == "prev":
        if prev is None:
            p = _init_exact(n)
        else:
            p = _interpolate_sequence(prev, n)
    else:
        p = _init_exact(n)

    return _quick_normalize(p)


def _fft_square(a, L=None):
    """Full linear convolution a*a via FFT, clipped to nonnegative."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    K = 2 * n - 1
    if n <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if L is None:
        L = _mp2(3 * n - 2)
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, L)
    c = c[:K]
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def _score_fft(a, L=None):
    """Score 2*n*max(conv(a,a))/sum(a)^2 using FFT."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n <= 0:
        return float("inf")
    c = _fft_square(a, L)
    S = float(a.sum())
    if S <= 1e-12:
        return float("inf")
    M = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * M / (S * S))


def _score_maxc(a, L=None):
    """Return (score, max convolution coefficient)."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n <= 0:
        return float("inf"), 0.0
    L = L if L is not None else _mp2(3 * n - 2)
    c = _fft_square(a, L)
    S = float(a.sum())
    M = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
    if S <= 1e-12:
        return float("inf"), M
    return float(2.0 * n * M / (S * S)), M


def _corr_from_fft(Fp, w, L, n):
    """
    Given Fp = rfft(p) and w of length 2n-1, compute h_i =
      sum_j p_j w_{i+j},
    for i=0..n-1.
    """
    if n <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], L)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(Fp * fw, L)
    h = conv[n - 1:2 * n - 1][::-1]
    return np.maximum(h, 0.0)


def soft_refine(p0, time_limit, rng, tag="soft", verbose=True):
    """
    Multiplicative/log-descent on the simplex for score minimization.
    """
    p0 = np.asarray(p0, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = p0.size
    if n <= 0:
        return p0, float("inf")

    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(1.0, float(time_limit))

    p = _normalize_positive(p0, 1e-12)
    L = _mp2(3 * n - 2)
    K = 2 * n - 1

    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = _score_fft(p, L)

    logp = np.log(p) - np.max(np.log(p))
    best_logp = logp.copy()

    gamma = 0.25
    gamma_min = 0.04
    gamma_max = 1.0
    prev_score = best_score
    last_improve = start
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        p = np.exp(logp - np.max(logp))
        p = p / p.sum()

        Fp = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
        c = np.fft.irfft(Fp * Fp, L)[:K]
        c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
        maxc = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
        S = float(p.sum())
        score = float(2.0 * n * maxc / (S * S)) if S > 1e-12 else float("inf")

        it += 1

        if score < best_score:
            best_score = score
            best_p = p.copy()
            best_logp = logp.copy()
            last_improve = time.monotonic()
            if verbose and (it <= 8 or it % 250 == 0):
                print(f"{tag} soft it={it} score={score:.7f}")

        if it > 1:
            if score < prev_score - 1e-13:
                gamma = min(gamma_max, gamma * 1.04)
            elif score > prev_score + 1e-13:
                gamma = max(gamma_min, gamma * 0.86)

            if score > best_score + 0.02:
                sig = 0.06
                logp = best_logp.copy() + rng.normal(0.0, sig, n)
                gamma = 0.25
                prev_score = best_score
                last_improve = time.monotonic()
                if verbose:
                    print(f"{tag} soft reset to best with noise")
                continue

        prev_score = score

        elapsed_frac = min(1.0, (time.monotonic() - start) / max(1e-9, time_limit))

        dead = time.monotonic() - last_improve
        if dead > max(2.0, 0.12 * time_limit) and it > 80:
            sig = 0.14 * (1.0 - 0.75 * elapsed_frac) + 0.02
            logp = best_logp.copy() + rng.normal(0.0, sig, n)
            gamma = 0.25
            prev_score = best_score
            last_improve = time.monotonic()
            if verbose:
                print(f"{tag} soft perturbed after {dead:.1f}s stale")
            continue

        if maxc > 0.0:
            rel_width = 0.25 - 0.23 * elapsed_frac * elapsed_frac
            rel_width = max(0.015, rel_width)
            logw = (c - maxc) / (rel_width * maxc)
            logw = np.nan_to_num(logw, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=-80.0)
            w = np.exp(logw)
            w = w + 1e-12
            sw = float(w.sum())
            if sw <= 1e-15 or not np.isfinite(sw):
                w = np.full(K, 1.0 / K, dtype=np.float64)
            else:
                w = w / sw
        else:
            w = np.full(K, 1.0 / K, dtype=np.float64)

        h = _corr_from_fft(Fp, w, L, n)
        if h is None or h.size != n:
            break

        target = float(np.dot(p, h))
        if not np.isfinite(target) or target <= 1e-15:
            target = float(h.mean())
        if target <= 1e-15:
            break

        dh = h / target - 1.0
        dh = np.clip(np.nan_to_num(dh, nan=0.0, posinf=1.0, neginf=-1.0), -1.0, 1.0)

        logp = logp - gamma * dh
        logp = logp - np.max(logp)
        min_log = logp.max() - 40.0
        logp = np.maximum(logp, min_log)
        logp = logp - np.mean(logp)

    if verbose:
        print(f"{tag} soft done best={best_score:.7f}")

    return best_p, best_score


def lp_refine(a0, time_limit, rng, tag="lp", verbose=True, row_cap=None, lp_timeout=5.0):
    """
    LP/linearization search around max(a*a)=1.
    Maximizes sum(delta) under linearized convolution constraints.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).ravel().copy()
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    if a.size == 0 or a.sum() <= 1e-15:
        return a, float("inf")

    n = a.size
    L = _mp2(3 * n - 2)
    K = 2 * n - 1

    if row_cap is None:
        if n <= 600:
            row_cap = min(K, 1000)
        elif n <= 1000:
            row_cap = min(K, 700)
        else:
            row_cap = min(K, 500)

    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(1.0, float(time_limit))

    cur_score, max0 = _score_maxc(a, L)
    if max0 > 0:
        a = a / np.sqrt(max0)
        cur_score, max0 = _score_maxc(a, L)

    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = cur_score
    last_improve = start
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0

    alphas = (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125, 0.0625, 0.03125, 0.015625, 0.0078125, 0.00390625)

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it += 1
        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remaining <= 1.5:
            break

        c = _fft_square(a, L)
        maxc0 = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
        S0 = float(a.sum())
        if maxc0 <= 1e-15 or S0 <= 1e-15:
            break

        cur_score = float(2.0 * n * maxc0 / (S0 * S0))
        a = a / np.sqrt(maxc0)
        c = c / maxc0
        S = S0 / np.sqrt(maxc0)
        cur_score = float(2.0 * n / (S * S)) if S > 1e-12 else cur_score

        if cur_score < best_score:
            best_score = cur_score
            best_a = a.copy()
            last_improve = time.monotonic()
            no_improve = 0
            if verbose and (it <= 5 or it % 25 == 0):
                print(f"{tag} LP cur/best={best_score:.7f} it={it}")

        if time.monotonic() - last_improve > max(60.0, 0.25 * time_limit):
            if verbose:
                print(f"{tag} LP stale, stopping")
            break

        if no_improve > 35 and time.monotonic() - last_improve > 25.0:
            if verbose:
                print(f"{tag} LP no progress, stopping")
            break

        if linprog is None:
            break

        tod = min(float(lp_timeout), max(1.5, remaining * 0.35))
        if remaining <= tod + 2.0:
            break

        m = int(min(row_cap, K))
        if m == K:
            idx = np.arange(K, dtype=np.int64)
        else:
            idx = np.argpartition(c, -m)[-m:].astype(np.int64)

        A = np.zeros((m, n), dtype=np.float64)
        b = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)

        for r, k in enumerate(idx):
            kk = int(k)
            j0 = kk - (n - 1)
            if j0 < 0:
                j0 = 0
            j1 = kk
            if j1 >= n:
                j1 = n - 1
            if j1 < j0:
                continue
            cnt = j1 - j0 + 1
            A[r, j0:j1 + 1] = 2.0 * a[kk - j1:kk - j0 + 1][::-1]
            slack = 1.0 - float(c[kk])
            b[r] = slack if slack > 0.0 else 0.0

        lower = -a
        upper_val = max(1e-6, 1.0 + float(a.max()))
        upper = np.full(n, upper_val, dtype=np.float64)
        bounds = np.column_stack((lower, upper))
        obj = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)

        try:
            res = linprog(
                obj,
                A_ub=A,
                b_ub=b,
                bounds=bounds,
                method="highs",
                options={"presolve": True, "time_limit": tod},
            )
        except Exception:
            res = None

        accepted = False
        if res is not None and getattr(res, "success", False) and res.x is not None:
            delta = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
            if np.all(np.isfinite(delta)):
                sdelta = float(delta.sum())
                if sdelta > 1e-11:
                    for alpha in alphas:
                        at = a + alpha * delta
                        if np.any(at < 0.0):
                            at = np.maximum(at, 0.0)
                        sat, maxat = _score_maxc(at, L)
                        if not np.isfinite(sat) or maxat <= 1e-15:
                            continue
                        if sat < cur_score - 1e-13:
                            a = at / np.sqrt(maxat)
                            if sat < best_score:
                                best_score = sat
                                best_a = a.copy()
                                last_improve = time.monotonic()
                                no_improve = 0
                                if verbose:
                                    print(f"{tag} LP accepted alpha={alpha:.6f} score={sat:.7f}")
                            else:
                                no_improve = 0
                            accepted = True
                            break

        if not accepted:
            no_improve += 1

        # As a light fallback inside LP, take one multiplicative step in simplex
        # coordinates if LP failed repeatedly.
        if no_improve >= 5 and time.monotonic() + 0.2 < deadline:
            p = a / a.sum()
            wp = np.full(K, 1e-12, dtype=np.float64)
            if maxc0 > 0:
                rel_width = 0.10
                logw = (c - 1.0) / rel_width
                logw = np.nan_to_num(logw, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=-60.0)
                wp = np.exp(logw) + 1e-12
                wp /= wp.sum()
            else:
                wp /= wp.sum()
            Fp = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
            h = _corr_from_fft(Fp, wp, L, n)
            tgt = float(np.dot(p, h))
            if tgt <= 1e-15:
                tgt = float(h.mean())
            if tgt > 1e-15:
                dh = h / tgt - 1.0
                dh = np.clip(np.nan_to_num(dh, nan=0.0, posinf=1.0, neginf=-1.0), -1.0, 1.0)
                logp = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
                logp = logp - 0.20 * dh
                logp = logp - np.max(logp)
                pp = np.exp(logp)
                pp = pp / pp.sum()
                spp, mp = _score_maxc(pp, L)
                if np.isfinite(spp) and mp > 0:
                    a = pp / np.sqrt(mp)
                    if spp < best_score:
                        best_score = spp
                        best_a = a.copy()
                        last_improve = time.monotonic()
                        no_improve = 0

    if verbose:
        print(f"{tag} LP done best={best_score:.7f}")

    return best_a, best_score


def local_polish(p0, time_limit, rng, tag="local", verbose=True):
    """Coordinate and random block hill-climbing polish."""
    p = np.asarray(p0, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = p.size
    if n <= 0:
        return p, float("inf")

    p = _quick_normalize(p)
    L = _mp2(3 * n - 2)
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = _score_fft(best_p, L)

    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(1.0, float(time_limit))

    factors = (0.0, 0.2, 0.4, 0.6, 0.8, 1.1, 1.4, 1.8, 3.0)
    sweep = 0
    last_improve = start

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        sweep += 1
        improved = False

        if n <= 800:
            # Periodic coordinate descent.
            if sweep % 3 == 1:
                for i in range(n):
                    if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                        break
                    orig = float(best_p[i])
                    for f in factors:
                        q = best_p.copy()
                        q[i] = orig * f
                        s = float(q.sum())
                        if s <= 1e-15:
                            continue
                        q /= s
                        sc = _score_fft(q, L)
                        if sc < best_score - 1e-13:
                            best_score = sc
                            best_p = q.copy()
                            improved = True
                            last_improve = time.monotonic()

        # Random blocks.
        n_blocks = 300 if n > 1000 else 900
        for _ in range(n_blocks):
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                break
            maxw = max(2, int(0.04 * n) + 1)
            w = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, maxw)))
            hi = max(1, n - w + 1)
            if hi <= 1:
                st = 0
            else:
                st = int(rng.integers(0, hi))
            q = best_p.copy()
            if rng.random() < 0.15:
                q[st:st + w] = 0.0
            else:
                frac = min(1.0, (time.monotonic() - start) / max(1e-9, time_limit))
                sig = 0.25 * max(0.15, 1.0 - frac)
                q[st:st + w] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sig, size=w))
            if rng.random() < 0.10:
                q = 0.5 * (q + q[::-1])
            s = float(q.sum())
            if s <= 1e-15:
                continue
            q /= s
            sc = _score_fft(q, L)
            if sc < best_score - 1e-13:
                best_score = sc
                best_p = q.copy()
                improved = True
                last_improve = time.monotonic()

        if not improved and time.monotonic() - last_improve > max(20.0, 0.5 * time_limit):
            break

    if verbose:
        print(f"{tag} local done best={best_score:.7f}")

    return best_p, best_score


def propose_candidate():
    """
    Main search driver._runs
    """
    total_start = time.monotonic()
    # Leave a safety margin under the 2400 second wall clock.
    deadline = total_start + 2330.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(20250101)

    # Load previous construction if provided by the environment.
    prev = None
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
        if arr.size > 0:
            arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
            arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
            if arr.sum() > 1e-12:
                prev = arr / arr.sum()
    except Exception:
        prev = None

    base_n = 500
    if prev is not None:
        base_n = int(prev.size)
        if base_n < 80:
            base_n = 500

    best_seq = None
    best_score = float("inf")

    def _maybe_update(seq, score):
        nonlocal best_seq, best_score
        if seq is not None and np.isfinite(score) and score < best_score:
            best_score = float(score)
            best_seq = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64).ravel().copy()
            print(f"*** NEW BEST score={best_score:.7f} n={best_seq.size}")

    # Quick inits at base and a few larger lengths to seed the search.
    quick_ns = []
    if base_n >= 50:
        quick_ns.append(base_n)
    for nx in (750, 1000, 1500, 2000, 2400):
        if nx >= 50:
            quick_ns.append(nx)
    quick_ns = sorted(set(quick_ns))

    for nx in quick_ns:
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 120.0:
            break
        kinds = ["prev", "exact", "power"] if prev is not None else ["exact", "power"]
        for kind in kinds:
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 60.0:
                break
            p = _get_initial(nx, kind, prev, rng)
            sc = _score_fft(p)
            _maybe_update(p, sc)

    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = _init_exact(500)
        best_score = _score_fft(best_seq)

    # Main refinement on the current best.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    n_best = int(best_seq.size)

    if rem > 250 and n_best <= 3000:
        t_soft0 = min(220.0, rem * 0.18)
        if t_soft0 > 40:
            seq, score = soft_refine(
                best_seq, t_soft0, rng, tag="mainSoft_pre", verbose=True
            )
            _maybe_update(seq, score)

    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 400 and n_best <= 1400:
        t_lp = min(1100.0, rem * 0.55)
        if t_lp > 120:
            seq, score = lp_refine(
                best_seq, t_lp, rng, tag="mainLP", verbose=True,
                row_cap=None, lp_timeout=6.0
            )
            _maybe_update(seq, score)

    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 220 and n_best <= 4000:
        t_soft = min(520.0, rem * 0.45)
        if t_soft > 60:
            seq, score = soft_refine(
                best_seq, t_soft, rng, tag="mainSoft", verbose=True
            )
            _maybe_update(seq, score)

    # Alternative starts and lengths.
    alt_ns = []
    for nx in (base_n, 600, 800, 1000, 1200, 1600, 2000, 2400, 3000):
        if 50 <= nx <= 3000:
            alt_ns.append(nx)
    alt_ns = sorted(set(alt_ns))

    for nx in alt_ns:
        rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem < 150:
            break
        kinds = []
        if prev is not None:
            kinds.append("prev")
        kinds.append("exact")
        if nx == base_n:
            kinds.append("power")
            kinds.append("random")
        else:
            kinds.append("power")

        for kind in kinds:
            rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
            if rem < 120:
                break
            p = _get_initial(nx, kind, prev, rng)
            sc0 = _score_fft(p)
            # Avoid spending time on obviously bad starts, but allow close ones.
            if sc0 > best_score + 0.08:
                continue

            t = min(380.0, rem * 0.28)
            if t < 50:
                break
            tag = f"alt_{kind}_{nx}"

            if nx <= 900:
                t1 = min(190.0, t * 0.55)
                seq1, score1 = lp_refine(
                    p, t1, rng, tag=tag + "_LP", verbose=False,
                    row_cap=None, lp_timeout=5.0
                )
                _maybe_update(seq1, score1)

                rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
                if rem > 70:
                    t2 = min(220.0, rem * 0.48)
                    seq2, score2 = soft_refine(
                        seq1, t2, rng, tag=tag + "_Soft", verbose=False
                    )
                    _maybe_update(seq2, score2)
            else:
                seq1, score1 = soft_refine(
                    p, t, rng, tag=tag + "_Soft", verbose=False
                )
                _maybe_update(seq1, score1)

    # Final polishing on the winner.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if best_seq is not None:
        n_best = int(best_seq.size)

        if rem > 120 and n_best <= 1200:
            t_loc = min(380.0, rem * 0.35)
            if t_loc > 40:
                seq, score = local_polish(
                    best_seq, t_loc, rng, tag="finalLocal", verbose=True
                )
                _maybe_update(seq, score)

        rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem > 160 and n_best <= 900:
            t_lp2 = min(600.0, rem * 0.55)
            if t_lp2 > 60:
                seq, score = lp_refine(
                    best_seq, t_lp2, rng, tag="finalLP", verbose=True,
                    row_cap=None, lp_timeout=8.0
                )
                _maybe_update(seq, score)

        rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem > 120 and n_best <= 2000:
            t_s2 = min(400.0, rem * 0.5)
            if t_s2 > 40:
                seq, score = soft_refine(
                    best_seq, t_s2, rng, tag="finalSoft", verbose=True
                )
                _maybe_update(seq, score)

    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = _init_exact(500)
        best_score = _score_fft(best_seq)

    # Final normalization to keep the evaluator happy. Score is scale invariant.
    seq = np.asarray(best_seq, dtype=np.float64).ravel().copy()
    seq[~np.isfinite(seq)] = 0.0
    seq = np.maximum(seq, 0.0)
    s = float(seq.sum())
    if s <= 1e-9:
        seq = _init_exact(500)
        seq = seq / seq.sum()
    else:
        seq = seq / s
    seq = np.minimum(seq, 1000.0)

    out = [float(x) for x in seq]

    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence(out)
        print(f"Proposed final verified score: {val}")
        if not np.isfinite(val):
            # Should not happen, but use a safe fallback.
            fb = _init_exact(500)
            fb = fb / fb.sum()
            out = [float(x) for x in fb]
            val2 = evaluate_sequence(out)
            print(f"Fallback verified score: {val2}")
    except Exception:
        print("evaluate_sequence verification not available in this context.")

    print(f"Final returned length: {len(out)}")
    print(f"Elapsed: {time.monotonic() - total_start:.1f}s")
    return out
```
